<div class="col-sm order-1">
      <h2 class="chapter_title">CHAPTER 16A. DEPARTMENT OF MANAGEMENT AND BUDGET</h2>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16A.01">
        <h1 class="shn">16A.01 DEPARTMENT OF MANAGEMENT AND BUDGET; COMMISSIONER; EMPLOYEES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.01.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Commissioner.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of management and budget manages the Department of Management and Budget, which may also be known as Minnesota Management and Budget. The commissioner is the state's controller and chief accounting and financial officer.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.01.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Appointment; qualification.</span></h2>
          <p>The governor appoints the commissioner under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/15.06">15.06</a>. The commissioner must have broad experience as an executive financial manager.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.01.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Deputy; confidential secretary.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may appoint a deputy and a confidential secretary. Each serves at the commissioner's pleasure in the unclassified service.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.01.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Organize, hire, delegate.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall:</p>
          <p>(1) organize the department;</p>
          <p>(2) hire the agents and classified civil service employees necessary to run the department;</p>
          <p>(3) define their duties; and</p>
          <p>(4) set conditions for, and control, delegation of the commissioner's powers, duties, and responsibilities to them.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16A.01.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/492/#laws.0.1.0">1973 c 492 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/305/#laws.0.8.0">1977 c 305 s 8</a>,9; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/628/#laws.2.1.0">1984 c 628 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.40.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 40</a>,109</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16A.011">
        <h1 class="shn">16A.011 DEFINITIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.011.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Applicability.</span></h2>
          <p>The definitions in this section apply to this chapter.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.011.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Agency.</span></h2>
          <p>Except when otherwise modified, "agency" includes an office, department, board, council, committee, authority, or commission of state government.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.011.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Allotment.</span></h2>
          <p>"Allotment" means a limit placed by the commissioner on the amount to be spent or encumbered during a period of time pursuant to an appropriation.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.011.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Appropriation.</span></h2>
          <p>"Appropriation" means an authorization by law to expend or encumber an amount in the treasury.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.011.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.</h2>
          <p>[Renumbered subd 17]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.011.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Biennium.</span></h2>
          <p>"Biennium" means a period of two consecutive fiscal years beginning in an odd-numbered calendar year and ending in the next odd-numbered calendar year.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.011.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Commissioner.</span></h2>
          <p>"Commissioner" means the commissioner of management and budget unless a different commissioner is specified.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.011.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Constitution.</span></h2>
          <p>"Constitution" means the state constitution.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.011.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Department.</span></h2>
          <p>Except in subdivision 2, "department" means the Department of Management and Budget unless a different department is specified.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.011.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Employee.</span></h2>
          <p>"Employee" includes elected officials, officers, and employees of the state, or agency, as the context requires.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.011.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Encumbrance.</span></h2>
          <p>"Encumbrance" means the commitment of a portion or all of an allotment in order to meet an obligation that is expected to be incurred to pay for goods or services received by the state or to pay a grant.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.011.12">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12.<span class="headnote">Executive agency.</span></h2>
          <p>"Executive agency" means an agency in the executive branch of state government.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.011.12a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12a.<span class="headnote">Executive branch state agency.</span></h2>
          <p>"Executive branch state agency" means an agency in the executive branch of state government, but does not include constitutional officers.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.011.13">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 13.<span class="headnote">Finance Committee.</span></h2>
          <p>"Finance Committee" means the Finance Committee of the senate.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.011.14">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 14.<span class="headnote">Fiscal year.</span></h2>
          <p>"Fiscal year" means the period beginning at midnight between June 30 and July 1 and ending 12 months later.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.011.14a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 14a.<span class="headnote">Statutory appropriation.</span></h2>
          <p>A statutory appropriation is one which sets apart a specified or unspecified and open amount of public money or funds of the state general fund for expenditure for a purpose and makes the amount, or a part of it, available for use continuously for a period of time beyond the end of the second fiscal year after the session of the legislature at which the appropriation is made.</p>
          <p>Every appropriation stated to be an "annual appropriation," "payable annually," "appropriated annually," or "annually appropriated," and every appropriation described by equivalent terms or language is a statutory appropriation as defined in this subdivision.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.011.15">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 15.</h2>
          <p>[Obsolete, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/112/#laws.2.50.0">2003 c 112 art 2 s 50</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.011.15a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 15a.<span class="headnote">Transfer.</span></h2>
          <p>A "transfer" means the authorization to move state money from one fund, account, or agency to another fund, account, or agency within the state treasury. When authorized by law, a transfer must reduce money in one fund, account, or agency and increase the same amount to a separate fund, account, or agency.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.011.16">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 16.<span class="headnote">Treasury.</span></h2>
          <p>Unless otherwise modified, "treasury" means the state treasury.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.011.17">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 17.<span class="headnote">Ways and Means Committee.</span></h2>
          <p>"Ways and Means Committee" means the chief fiscal committee of the house of representatives.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16A.011.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">(<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1940A/cite/3">48</a>) <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1913/0/140/#laws.0.1.0">1913 c 140 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/399/#laws.0.1.0">1969 c 399 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/597/#laws.0.31.0">1984 c 597 s 31</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/628/#laws.2.1.0">1984 c 628 art 2 s 1</a>; art 6 s 1; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/469/#laws.1.1.0">1988 c 469 art 1 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/192/#laws.0.43.0">1993 c 192 s 43</a>-45; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/395/#laws.0.4.0">2000 c 395 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/284/#laws.2.1.0">2004 c 284 art 2 s 1</a>,19; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.109.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 109</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/62/#laws.2.49.0">2023 c 62 art 2 s 49</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">WARRANTS; GIFTS; INVESTMENTS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16A.012">
        <h1 class="shn">16A.012 WARRANTS; DISCOUNTS.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner of management and budget shall in no case purchase, redeem, or receive any warrant at less than its face value, nor receive any fee or reward for transacting any official duty, other than the salary provided by law. If the public revenue shall suffer loss by reason of the commissioner's failure to call delinquents to account when required to do so by law, the commissioner shall be accountable for all sums due from such delinquents as if the same had been paid.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16A.012.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1905/cite/4">RL s 46</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/112/#laws.2.50.0">2003 c 112 art 2 s 50</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.109.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 109</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16A.013">
        <h1 class="shn">16A.013 GIFTS; ACCEPTANCE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.013.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Procedure.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of management and budget is authorized to receive and accept, on behalf of the state, any gift, bequest, devise, or endowment which may be made by any person, by will, deed, gift, or otherwise, to or for the benefit of the state, or any of its departments or agencies, or to or in aid, or for the benefit, support, or maintenance of any educational, charitable, or other institution maintained in whole or in part by the state, or for the benefit of students, employees, or inmates thereof, or for any proper state purpose or function, and the money, property, or funds constituting such gift, bequest, devise, or endowment. No such gift, bequest, devise, or endowment shall be so accepted unless the commissioner of management and budget determines that it is for the interest of the state to accept it, and approve of and direct the acceptance. If a gift, bequest, devise, or endowment is money or other negotiable instruments, then the deposit of it does not constitute acceptance. In the event that the money or other negotiable instruments are deposited but not approved, the amount deposited must be refunded. When, in order to effect the purpose for which any gift, bequest, devise, or endowment has been accepted, it is necessary to sell property so received, the commissioner of management and budget, upon request of the authority in charge of the agency, department, or institution concerned, may sell it at a price which shall be fixed by the State Board of Investment.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.013.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Charitable trusts; administration.</span></h2>
          <p>When a charitable trust is created by will or otherwise for the benefit of the state or any of its departments or agencies or to or in aid, or for the benefit, support or maintenance of any educational, charitable, or other institution maintained in whole or in part by the state, or for the benefit of students, employees, or inmates thereof and any officer or employee of the state or any of its departments or agencies is named in the trust instrument as trustee, it shall be presumed that such trust is a gift to be administered under this section and the courts shall construe the instrument creating the trust accordingly.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.013.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Gift subject to contract.</span></h2>
          <p>Whenever the gift, bequest, devise, or endowment referred to in subdivisions 1 and 2 consists of real property, or an interest therein, which is subject to a contract for the conveyance thereof made by the donor or a predecessor in interest with another, or of the vendor's interest, or some portion thereof, in such a contract for conveyance, the commissioner of management and budget is authorized, on behalf of and in the name of the state of Minnesota, upon receipt from the vendee under such contract for conveyance, or the vendee's personal representatives or assigns, of such amounts as are due the state or the department, agency, or institution involved, to execute a deed conveying to such vendee, or the vendee's personal representatives or assigns, all the right, title, and interest of the state of Minnesota in and to the real property involved.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.013.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Termination of contract.</span></h2>
          <p>In case of default by the purchaser, or the purchaser's personal representatives or assigns, in the conditions of any such contract for the conveyance of real estate, the commissioner of management and budget is authorized, in the name of the state of Minnesota, to terminate such contract under and pursuant to the provisions of Minnesota Statutes 1941, section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/559.21">559.21</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.013.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Previous gifts.</span></h2>
          <p>The provisions of subdivisions 3 and 4 apply to gifts, bequests, devises, or endowments heretofore made.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16A.013.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1907/0/170/#laws.0.1.0">1907 c 170 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1941/0/353/#laws.0.1.0">1941 c 353 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1945/0/359/#laws.0.1.0">1945 c 359 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/492/#laws.0.14.0">1973 c 492 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/301/#laws.0.63.0">1983 c 301 s 63</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/254/#laws.1.39.0">1995 c 254 art 1 s 39</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/10/#laws.2.15.0">1Sp2001 c 10 art 2 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/112/#laws.2.50.0">2003 c 112 art 2 s 50</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.109.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 109</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16A.014">
        <h1 class="shn">16A.014 ADMINISTRATION OF GIFTS.</h1>
        <p>In case any such gift, bequest, devise, or endowment is so accepted, the same and the proceeds thereof shall be administered and applied according to the terms of the will, deed of gift, or other instrument defining, providing for, creating, or establishing the same; but all such property and funds shall be held by the commissioner of management and budget in an official capacity and paid out and disbursed the same as other state funds.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16A.014.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1907/0/170/#laws.0.2.0">1907 c 170 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/112/#laws.2.50.0">2003 c 112 art 2 s 50</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.109.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 109</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16A.015">
        <h1 class="shn">16A.015 INVESTMENT OF GIFTS.</h1>
        <p>In case it is provided by the terms of such will, deed of gift, or other instrument that the capital of the money, property, or fund constituting such gift, bequest, devise, or endowment, or any part of such capital, shall be kept invested, the same shall be invested and kept invested in the same manner and by the same officers or body as the school funds of the state are by law required to be invested.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16A.015.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1907/0/170/#laws.0.3.0">1907 c 170 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/112/#laws.2.50.0">2003 c 112 art 2 s 50</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16A.016">
        <h1 class="shn">16A.016 PAYMENT; APPROPRIATION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.016.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Payment; expenditure.</span></h2>
          <p>In the event such gift, bequest, devise, or endowment is made or designated by the donor for a certain institution, department, or agency, the commissioner of management and budget shall, from time to time, pay out in the usual manner, upon the order of the board, commission, or other body charged with the direct and immediate supervision, control, or management of the designated institution, department, or agency, all money which may become available for such purpose under the terms of such will, deed or gift, or other instrument; and the same shall be expended and applied by this board, commission, or other body as nearly as may be in accordance with the terms and conditions of such gift, bequest, devise, or endowment.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.016.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Appropriation.</span></h2>
          <p>There is hereby appropriated from the fund in the state treasury created under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.013">16A.013</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.015">16A.015</a>, to which such money was credited to such person, department, agency, or institution as is entitled to such payment an amount sufficient to carry out the terms and conditions of such gift, bequest, devise, or endowment.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16A.016.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1907/0/170/#laws.0.4.0">1907 c 170 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1941/0/353/#laws.0.2.0">1941 c 353 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1959/0/158/#laws.0.1.0">1959 c 158 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/112/#laws.2.50.0">2003 c 112 art 2 s 50</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.109.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 109</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.16A.02"><b>16A.02</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/628/#laws.2.4.0">1984 c 628 art 2 s 4</a>]</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">COMMISSIONER'S DUTIES; AUTHORITY</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16A.04">
        <h1 class="shn">16A.04 BUDGET AND CASH PROJECTION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.04.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">To prepare, consult, supervise.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall prepare the biennial budget with projections of revenues and expenditures for both the biennial budget period and the biennium following the biennial budget period. The governor shall supervise the preparation unless there is a governor-elect, who then shall provide the supervision.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.04.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/628/#laws.2.4.0">1984 c 628 art 2 s 4</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.04.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/628/#laws.2.4.0">1984 c 628 art 2 s 4</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.04.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p>[Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.041">16A.041</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16A.04.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/492/#laws.0.4.0">1973 c 492 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/628/#laws.2.1.0">1984 c 628 art 2 s 1</a>; art 6 s 1; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/654/#laws.2.51.0">1984 c 654 art 2 s 51</a>,52; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/192/#laws.0.46.0">1993 c 192 s 46</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16A.041">
        <h1 class="shn">16A.041 RULEMAKING.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner may make rules on the powers, duties, and responsibilities given to the department or the commissioner under state law.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16A.041.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/492/#laws.0.4.0">1973 c 492 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/628/#laws.2.1.0">1984 c 628 art 2 s 1</a>; art 6 s 1; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/654/#laws.2.51.0">1984 c 654 art 2 s 51</a>,52</p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.16A.05"><b>16A.05</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/410/#laws.0.19.0">1977 c 410 s 19</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16A.055">
        <h1 class="shn">16A.055 COMMISSIONER'S DUTIES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.055.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">List.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner shall:</p>
          <p>(1) receive and record all money paid into the state treasury and safely keep it until lawfully paid out;</p>
          <p>(2) manage the state's financial affairs;</p>
          <p>(3) keep the state's general account books according to generally accepted government accounting principles;</p>
          <p>(4) keep expenditure and revenue accounts according to generally accepted government accounting principles;</p>
          <p>(5) develop, provide instructions for, prescribe, and manage a state uniform accounting system; and</p>
          <p>(6) provide to the state the expertise to ensure that all state funds are accounted for under generally accepted government accounting principles.</p>
          <p>(b) In addition to the duties in paragraph (a), the commissioner has the powers and duties given to the commissioner in chapter 43A.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.055.1a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1a.<span class="headnote">Program evaluation and organizational development services.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may assist state agencies by providing analytical, statistical, program evaluation using experimental or quasi-experimental design, and organizational development services to state agencies in order to assist the agency to achieve the agency's mission and to operate efficiently and effectively. For purposes of this section, "experimental design" means a method of evaluating the impact of a service that uses random assignment to assign participants into groups that respectively receive the studied service and those that receive service as usual, so that any difference in outcomes found at the end of the evaluation can be attributed to the studied service; and "quasi-experimental design" means a method of evaluating the impact of a service that uses strategies other than random assignment to establish statistically similar groups that respectively receive the service and those that receive service as usual, so that any difference in outcomes found at the end of the evaluation can be attributed to the studied service.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.055.1b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1b.<span class="headnote">Consultation to develop performance measures for grants.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner must, in consultation with the commissioners of health, human services, and children, youth, and families, develop an ongoing consultation schedule to create, review, and revise, as necessary, performance measures, data collection, and program evaluation plans for all state-funded grants administered by the commissioners of health, human services, and children, youth, and families that distribute at least $1,000,000 annually.</p>
          <p>(b) Following the development of the ongoing consultation schedule under paragraph (a), the commissioner and the commissioner of the administering agency must conduct a grant program consultation in accordance with the ongoing consultation schedule. Each grant program consultation must include a review of performance measures, data collection, program evaluation plans, and reporting for each grant program. Following each consultation, the commissioner and the commissioner of the administering agency may revise evaluation metrics of a grant program. The commissioner may provide continuing support to the grant program in accordance with subdivision 1a.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.055.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Accounting system required.</span></h2>
          <p>An agency must use the uniform accounting system prescribed by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.055.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Access to records.</span></h2>
          <p>An agency must give the commissioner or a designee of the commissioner free access to its financial documents.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.055.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Commissioner's designee.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may assign a designee to an agency to monitor its financial activities and to ensure compliance with statutes and administrative requirements promulgated by the commissioner. The designee may assist the agency as the commissioner considers appropriate. The agency's head shall supervise its employees and develop a budget consistent with its goals, responsibilities, and priorities.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.055.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Retirement fund reporting.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The commissioner may not require a public retirement fund to use financial or actuarial reporting practices or procedures different from those required by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/356.20">356.20</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/356.215">356.215</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.055.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Mission; efficiency.</span></h2>
          <p>It is part of the department's mission that within the department's resources the commissioner shall endeavor to:</p>
          <p>(1) prevent the waste or unnecessary spending of public money;</p>
          <p>(2) use innovative fiscal and human resource practices to manage the state's resources and operate the department as efficiently as possible;</p>
          <p>(3) coordinate the department's activities wherever appropriate with the activities of other governmental agencies;</p>
          <p>(4) use technology where appropriate to increase agency productivity, improve customer service, increase public access to information about government, and increase public participation in the business of government;</p>
          <p>(5) utilize constructive and cooperative labor-management practices to the extent otherwise required by chapters 43A and 179A;</p>
          <p>(6) report to the legislature on the performance of agency operations and the accomplishment of agency goals in the agency's biennial budget according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.10#stat.16A.10.1">16A.10, subdivision 1</a>; and</p>
          <p>(7) recommend to the legislature appropriate changes in law necessary to carry out the mission and improve the performance of the department.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.055.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Grant acceptance.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may apply for and receive grants from any source for the purpose of fulfilling any of the duties of the department. All funds received under this subdivision are appropriated to the commissioner for the purposes for which the funds are received.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16A.055.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">(<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1940A/cite/4">80-2</a>) <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1939/0/431/#laws.3.1.0">1939 c 431 art 3 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1955/0/863/#laws.0.15.0">1955 c 863 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/492/#laws.0.3.0">1973 c 492 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/231/#laws.0.3.0">1976 c 231 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1979/0/314/#laws.0.1.0">1979 c 314 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/628/#laws.2.1.0">1984 c 628 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/13/#laws.0.95.0">1Sp1985 c 13 s 95</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/351/#laws.0.14.0">1989 c 351 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/192/#laws.0.47.0">1993 c 192 s 47</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/248/#laws.11.1.0">1995 c 248 art 11 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/457/#laws.0.2.0">1996 c 457 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/366/#laws.0.20.0">1998 c 366 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/461/#laws.1.2.0">2000 c 461 art 1 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/271/#laws.3.47.0">2006 c 271 art 3 s 47</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/204/#laws.0.4.0">2008 c 204 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/349/#laws.10.1.0">2008 c 349 art 10 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.41.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 41</a>,42; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/9/#laws.2.6.0">1Sp2019 c 9 art 2 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/62/#laws.2.50.0">2023 c 62 art 2 s 50</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/127/#laws.66.1.0">2024 c 127 art 66 s 1</a>,2</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16A.056">
        <h1 class="shn">16A.056 WEBSITE WITH SEARCHABLE DATABASE ON STATE EXPENDITURES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.056.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Web database requirement.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner, in consultation with the commissioners of administration and revenue and the legislative auditor, must maintain a website with a searchable database providing the public with information on state contracts, state appropriations, state expenditures, state tax expenditures, and state entities that are the subject of audits. The website must not include information that is not public data, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.02#stat.13.02.8a">13.02, subdivision 8a</a>. For each data field identified in subdivisions 2 to 6, the searchable database must allow a user of the website to:</p>
          <p>(1) perform a search using that field;</p>
          <p>(2) sort by that field;</p>
          <p>(3) obtain information grouped or aggregated by that field, where groups or subtotals are feasible; and</p>
          <p>(4) view information in that field by each fiscal year.</p>
          <p>The searchable database may accommodate grouping and aggregating by allowing the user to download the data into a user-controlled database.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.056.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Contracts.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The searchable database on the website must include at least the following data fields on state contracts:</p>
          <p>(1) the name of the entity receiving the contract;</p>
          <p>(2) the name of the agency entering into the contract;</p>
          <p>(3) an indication if the contract is for (i) goods; (ii) professional or technical services; (iii) services other than professional and technical services; or (iv) a grant; and</p>
          <p>(4) the fund or funds from which the entity receiving the contract will be paid.</p>
          <p>(b) For each contract, the database must also include:</p>
          <p>(1) an address for each entity receiving a contract; and</p>
          <p>(2) a brief statement of the purpose of the contract or grant.</p>
          <p>(c) Information on a new contract or grant must be entered into the database within 30 days after the contract or grant is entered into.</p>
          <p>(d) For purposes of this section, a "grant" is a contract between a state agency and a recipient, the primary purpose of which is to transfer cash or a thing of value to the recipient to support a public purpose. Grant does not include aid payments to units of local government, payments to state employees, or payments made under laws providing for assistance to individuals.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.056.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Appropriations.</span></h2>
          <p>The searchable database on the website must include at least the following data fields on state appropriations:</p>
          <p>(1) the agency receiving the appropriation, or the name of the nonstate entity receiving state money;</p>
          <p>(2) the agency program, to the extent applicable;</p>
          <p>(3) the agency activity, to the extent applicable;</p>
          <p>(4) an item within an activity if applicable;</p>
          <p>(5) the fund from which the appropriation is made; and</p>
          <p>(6) the object of expenditure.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.056.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">State expenditures.</span></h2>
          <p>The searchable database on the website must include at least the following data fields on state expenditures:</p>
          <p>(1) the name of the agency or nonstate entity making the expenditure;</p>
          <p>(2) the agency program, to the extent applicable;</p>
          <p>(3) the agency activity, to the extent applicable;</p>
          <p>(4) an item within an activity if applicable;</p>
          <p>(5) the fund from which the expenditure is made; and</p>
          <p>(6) the object of expenditure.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.056.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Tax expenditures.</span></h2>
          <p>The website must include a searchable database of state tax expenditures. For each fiscal year, the database must include data fields showing the estimated impact on state revenues of each tax expenditure item listed in the report prepared under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/270C.11">270C.11</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.056.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Audits.</span></h2>
          <p>The website required by this section must include a link to a website containing the findings and results from the audits completed by the legislative auditor that have been released to the public.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.056.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Retention of data.</span></h2>
          <p>The database required under this section must include information beginning with fiscal year 2012 appropriations and must retain data for at least ten years.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.056.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Consultation.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of management and budget must consult with the chairs of the house of representatives Ways and Means and senate Finance Committees before encumbering any money appropriated on or after July 1, 2009, for the planning, development, and implementation of state accounting or procurement systems. No money appropriated for these purposes may be spent unless the commissioner certifies that the systems will allow compliance with requirements of this section.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16A.056.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.43.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 43</a>,109; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/134/#laws.0.15.0">2013 c 134 s 15</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16A.0561">
        <h1 class="shn">16A.0561 MAPPED DATA ON EXPENDITURES.</h1>
        <p>(a) Data on expenditure of money from the funds as specified under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/3.303#stat.3.303.10">3.303, subdivision 10</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116P.08">116P.08</a> may, if practicable, be made available on the web in a manner that allows the public to obtain information about a project receiving an appropriation by clicking on a map. To the extent feasible, the map should include or link to information about each project, including, but not limited to, the location, the name of the entity receiving the appropriation, the source of the appropriation, the amount of money received, and a general statement of the purpose of the appropriation.</p>
        <p>(b) If requested, the Legislative Coordinating Commission may, to the extent practicable, provide relevant executive branch agencies with public geospatial data that it receives for its website required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/3.303#stat.3.303.10">3.303, subdivision 10</a>. The commissioner may make this information available to the public in a similar manner as information provided under paragraph (a).</p>
        <p>(c) In creating plans for public expenditures from all geographically locatable or project based appropriations, prospective budget and project planning should consider geographic and data reporting that would facilitate the goals of this section.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16A.0561.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/392/#laws.1.2.0">2010 c 392 art 1 s 2</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16A.057">
        <h1 class="shn">16A.057 INTERNAL CONTROLS AND INTERNAL AUDITING.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.057.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Establishment of system.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner is responsible for the system of internal controls across the executive branch. The commissioner must coordinate the design, implementation, and maintenance of an effective system of internal controls and internal auditing for all executive agencies. The system must:</p>
          <p>(1) safeguard public funds and assets and minimize incidences of fraud, waste, and abuse;</p>
          <p>(2) ensure that programs are administered in compliance with federal and state laws and rules; and</p>
          <p>(3) require documentation of internal control procedures over financial management activities, provide for analysis of risks, and provide for periodic evaluation of control procedures to satisfy the commissioner that these procedures are adequately designed, properly implemented, and functioning effectively.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.057.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Standards.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner must adopt internal control standards and policies that agencies must follow to meet the requirements of subdivision 1. These standards and policies may include separation of duties, safeguarding receipts, time entry, approval of travel, and other topics the commissioner determines are necessary to comply with subdivision 1.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.057.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Training and assistance.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall coordinate training for accounting personnel and financial managers in state agencies on internal controls as necessary to ensure financial integrity in the state's financial transactions. The commissioner shall provide internal control support to agencies that the commissioner determines need this assistance.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.057.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Sharing internal audit resources.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner must administer a program for sharing internal auditors among executive agencies that do not have their own internal auditors and for assembling interagency teams of internal auditors as necessary.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.057.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Monitoring Office of the Legislative Auditor audits.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner must review audit reports from the Office of the Legislative Auditor and take appropriate steps to address internal control problems found in executive agencies.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.057.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Budget for internal controls.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of management and budget may require that each executive agency spend a specified percentage of its operating budget on internal control systems. The commissioner of management and budget may require that an agency transfer a portion of its operating budget to the commissioner to pay for internal control functions performed by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.057.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Annual report. </span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner must report to the legislative audit commission and the governor by January 31 of each odd-numbered year on the system of internal controls and internal auditing in executive agencies.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.057.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Agency head responsibilities.</span></h2>
          <p>The head of each executive agency is responsible for designing, implementing, and maintaining an effective internal control system within the agency that complies with the requirements of subdivision 1, clauses (1) to (3). The head of each executive agency must annually certify that the agency head has reviewed the agency's internal control systems, and that these systems are in compliance with standards and policies established by the commissioner. The agency head must submit the signed certification form to the commissioner of management and budget, in a form specified by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.057.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">State colleges and universities.</span></h2>
          <p>This section does not apply to the Minnesota State Colleges and Universities system.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16A.057.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.44.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 44</a>,109</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16A.06">
        <h1 class="shn">16A.06 ADDITIONAL COMMISSIONER DUTIES AND POWERS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.06.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Agency to comply.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner has the duties and powers stated in this section. An executive agency must do what the commissioner requires of it under this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.06.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Financial reports.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner from time to time shall require an executive agency to prepare financial reports on department forms so the administration and the legislature can compare spending plans with appropriations for programs and activities.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.06.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Evaluate and compare costs.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall provide a system to measure the effect of fund expenditures so as to evaluate and compare the cost of functions or programs.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.06.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Reporting agency performance.</span></h2>
          <p>Executive agencies shall prepare performance-based budget plans according to schedules, forms, and standards as established by the commissioner. The commissioner may also require other periodic reports of agency performance.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.06.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Estimates.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner from time to time shall require an executive agency to report estimates of its income and receipts. The commissioner shall use the estimates to evaluate the state's financial condition.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.06.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Report on financial affairs.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall, when directed, report on the state's financial affairs to the governor.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.06.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Information for policy making.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall obtain from an executive agency any information needed to make state financial policy.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.06.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/632/#laws.3.65.0">1994 c 632 art 3 s 65</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.06.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/134/#laws.0.31.0">2013 c 134 s 31</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.06.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/112/#laws.1.19.0">2003 c 112 art 1 s 19</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.06.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Permanent school fund reporting.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The commissioner shall annually report to the Legislative Permanent School Fund Commission and the legislature the amount of the permanent school fund transfer and information about the investment of the permanent school fund provided by the State Board of Investment. The State Board of Investment shall provide information about how they maximized the long-term economic return of the permanent school fund.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.06.12">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12.<span class="headnote">Audit of state's use of federal funds.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall contract with a qualified auditor to conduct the annual audit required by the Single Audit Act of 1984, Public Law 98-502; the Single Audit Act Amendments of 1996, Public Law 104-156; and Code of Federal Regulations, title 2, part 200, Uniform Administrative Requirements, Cost Principles, and Audit Requirements for Federal Awards (Uniform Guidance). The cost of the audit shall be billed to the agencies audited during the subsequent fiscal year. Amounts paid by state agencies shall be deposited in the general fund.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16A.06.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/492/#laws.0.6.0">1973 c 492 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/582/#laws.0.3.0">1973 c 582 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/628/#laws.2.1.0">1984 c 628 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/654/#laws.2.53.0">1984 c 654 art 2 s 53</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/275/#laws.0.2.0">1987 c 275 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/192/#laws.0.48.0">1993 c 192 s 48</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/438/#laws.4.1.0">1996 c 438 art 4 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/10/#laws.2.20.0">1Sp2001 c 10 art 2 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/357/#laws.0.1.0">2008 c 357 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/96/#laws.1.1.0">2009 c 96 art 1 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.109.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 109</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/249/#laws.0.2.0">2012 c 249 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/286/#laws.8.1.0">2012 c 286 art 8 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/298/#laws.0.7.0">2012 c 298 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/12/#laws.2.4.0">1Sp2021 c 12 art 2 s 4</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16A.061">
        <h1 class="shn">16A.061 MAY ISSUE COMMEMORATIVE MEDALLIONS.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner of management and budget may issue medallions to commemorate popular contemporaneous events of statewide interest.</p>
        <p>The commissioner of management and budget may make reasonable arrangements with public or private entities for the production, distribution, marketing, and sale of the medallions. The commissioner of management and budget or other entity may solicit and receive nonstate funds or in-kind contributions in connection with any part of the medallion program. Proceeds from sales, nonstate funds, and in-kind contributions must be deposited in a dedicated account.</p>
        <p>Money in the account is appropriated to the commissioner of management and budget for purposes of the program. Any profit earned on the sale of the medallions must be used for grants to support the event for which the medallions were issued. The state grant must be matched by an equal amount from private sources.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16A.061.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/345/#laws.1.41.0">1991 c 345 art 1 s 41</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/112/#laws.2.50.0">2003 c 112 art 2 s 50</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.109.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 109</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16A.065">
        <h1 class="shn">16A.065 PREPAY SOFTWARE, SUBSCRIPTIONS, UNITED STATES DOCUMENTS.</h1>
        <p>Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.41#stat.16A.41.1">16A.41, subdivision 1</a>, the commissioner may allow an agency to make advance deposits or payments for software or software maintenance services for state-owned or leased electronic data processing equipment, for information technology hosting services, for sole source maintenance agreements where it is not cost-effective to pay in arrears, for exhibit booth space or boat slip rental when required by the renter to guarantee the availability of space, for registration fees where advance payment is required or advance payment discount is provided, for newspaper, magazine, and other subscription fees, and other costs where advance payment discount is provided or are customarily paid for in advance. The commissioner may also allow advance deposits by any department with the Library of Congress and federal Supervisor of Documents for items to be purchased from those federal agencies.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16A.065.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/614/#laws.0.54.0">1980 c 614 s 54</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/544/#laws.0.4.0">1984 c 544 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/628/#laws.2.1.0">1984 c 628 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/654/#laws.2.54.0">1984 c 654 art 2 s 54</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/248/#laws.0.6.0">1985 c 248 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/271/#laws.0.1.0">1989 c 271 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/192/#laws.0.49.0">1993 c 192 s 49</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/181/#laws.0.1.0">2006 c 181 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/77/#laws.2.6.0">2015 c 77 art 2 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/10/#laws.3.3.0">1Sp2019 c 10 art 3 s 3</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16A.067">
        <h1 class="shn">16A.067 TAXPAYER RECEIPT.</h1>
        <p>(a) The commissioner, in consultation with the commissioner of revenue, must develop and publish on the Department of Management and Budget's website an interactive taxpayer receipt in accordance with this section. The receipt must describe the share of state general fund expenditures represented by major expenditure categories in the most recent fiscal year for which data is available. The receipt must show the approximate allocation of motor vehicle fuel taxes among eligible transportation purposes.</p>
        <p>(b) For each expenditure category, the receipt must include select data on the performance goals and outcomes for the category, based on the goals and outcomes data required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.10#stat.16A.10.1b">16A.10, subdivision 1b</a>.</p>
        <p>(c) The website must allow a user to input an income amount, and must estimate the amount of major state taxes paid by the user. The website must allocate the user's estimated state tax liability to each major expenditure category based on the category's percentage share of total state general fund spending. For the purposes of this section, "major state taxes" means income, sales, alcohol, tobacco, and motor vehicle fuels taxes.</p>
        <p>(d) Using the income amount entered by the user, the website must estimate the amount of income and direct sales taxes paid based upon the taxpayer's income. The website must allow a user to indicate whether the user used tobacco, consumed alcohol, or purchased motor vehicle fuel in the previous year, and provide a corresponding estimate of the cigarette, alcohol, and motor vehicle fuel taxes paid by the user.</p>
        <p>(e) The commissioner of management and budget, in consultation with the commissioner of revenue, must update the receipt by December 31 of each year, and must annually promote to the public the availability of the website.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16A.067.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/64/#laws.15.3.0">2023 c 64 art 15 s 3</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.16A.07"><b>16A.07</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/628/#laws.2.4.0">1984 c 628 art 2 s 4</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.16A.08"><b>16A.08</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/628/#laws.2.4.0">1984 c 628 art 2 s 4</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.16A.09"><b>16A.09</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/231/#laws.0.34.0">1976 c 231 s 34</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16A.091">
        <h1 class="shn">16A.091 ACCOUNTABILITY AND PERFORMANCE MANAGEMENT.</h1>
        <p>(a) The commissioner of management and budget is responsible for the coordination, development, assessment, and communication of information, performance measures, planning, and policy concerning the state's future.</p>
        <p>(b) The commissioner must develop a statewide system of economic, social, and environmental performance measures. The commissioner must provide information to assist public and elected officials with understanding the status of these performance measures.</p>
        <p>(c) The commissioner may appoint one deputy with principal responsibility for planning, strategy, and performance management.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16A.091.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/62/#laws.2.51.0">2023 c 62 art 2 s 51</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">STATE BUDGET</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16A.095">
        <h1 class="shn">16A.095 STATE BUDGET SYSTEM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.095.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.</h2>
          <p>MS 1976 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/455/#laws.0.95.0">1977 c 455 s 95</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.095.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Rules and instructions.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall make rules and instructions for budget preparation. They must deal with classifying expenditures and with the content and submission of budget requests and performance measures for each budget activity.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.095.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Budget improvements.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may choose executive agencies to test improvements in the budget system. The commissioner shall recommend required legislation to install improvements in the budget system for all agencies that submit budget information in the system. The budget system must classify expenditures by programs and budget activities and, to the greatest extent practicable, emphasize alternative approaches in program development and criteria to evaluate and measure performance.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.095.2a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2a.<span class="headnote">Mutual cooperation; due regard.</span></h2>
          <p>Executive agencies must cooperate with the commissioner in preparing the budget. The budget must meet the commissioner's requirements while giving due regard to the executive agencies' requirements.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.095.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/192/#laws.0.110.0">1993 c 192 s 110</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16A.095.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/231/#laws.0.4.0">1976 c 231 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/455/#laws.0.71.0">1977 c 455 s 71</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/628/#laws.2.1.0">1984 c 628 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/134/#laws.0.16.0">2013 c 134 s 16</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16A.10">
        <h1 class="shn">16A.10 BUDGET PREPARATION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.10.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Budget format.</span></h2>
          <p>In each even-numbered calendar year the commissioner shall prepare budget forms and instructions for all agencies, including guidelines for reporting agency performance measures, subject to the approval of the governor. The commissioner shall request and receive advisory recommendations from the chairs of the senate Finance Committee and house of representatives Ways and Means Committee before adopting a format for the biennial budget document. By June 15, the commissioner shall send the proposed budget forms to the appropriations and finance committees. The committees have until July 15 to give the commissioner their advisory recommendations on possible improvements. To facilitate this consultation, the commissioner shall establish a working group consisting of executive branch staff and designees of the chairs of the senate Finance and house of representatives Ways and Means Committees. The commissioner must involve this group in all stages of development of budget forms and instructions. The budget format must show actual expenditures and receipts for the most recent fiscal year, estimated expenditures and receipts for the current fiscal year, and estimates for each fiscal year of the next biennium. Estimated expenditures must be classified by funds and character of expenditures and subclassified by programs and activities. Agency revenue estimates must have supporting documentation to show how the estimates were made and what factors were used. Receipts must be classified by funds, programs, and activities. Expenditure and revenue estimates must be based on the law in existence at the time the estimates are prepared.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.10.1a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1a.<span class="headnote">Purpose of performance data.</span></h2>
          <p>Performance data shall be presented in the budget proposal to:</p>
          <p>(1) provide information so that the legislature can determine the extent to which state programs are successful;</p>
          <p>(2) encourage agencies to develop clear goals and objectives for their programs; and</p>
          <p>(3) strengthen accountability to Minnesotans by providing a record of state government's performance in providing effective and efficient services.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.10.1b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1b.<span class="headnote">Performance data format.</span></h2>
          <p>Agencies shall present performance data that measures the performance of programs in meeting program goals and objectives. Measures reported may include indicators of outputs, efficiency, outcomes, and other measures relevant to understanding each program. Agencies shall present as much historical information as needed to understand major trends and shall set targets for future performance issues where feasible and appropriate. The information shall appropriately highlight agency performance issues that would assist legislative review and decision making.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.10.1c">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1c.<span class="headnote">Performance measures for change items.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">For each change item in the budget proposal requesting new or increased funding, the budget document must present proposed performance measures that can be used to determine if the new or increased funding is accomplishing its goals. To the extent possible, each budget change item must identify relevant statewide goals and indicators related to the proposed initiative.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.10.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">By October 15 and November 30.</span></h2>
          <p>By October 15 of each even-numbered year, an agency must file the following with the commissioner:</p>
          <p>(1) budget estimates for the most recent and current fiscal years;</p>
          <p>(2) its upcoming biennial budget estimates;</p>
          <p>(3) a comprehensive and integrated statement of agency missions and outcome and performance measures; and</p>
          <p>(4) a concise explanation of any planned changes in the level of services or new activities.</p>
          <p>The commissioner shall prepare and file the budget estimates for an agency failing to file them. By November 30, the commissioner shall send the final budget format, agency budget estimates for the next biennium, and copies of the filed material to the Ways and Means and Finance Committees, except that the commissioner shall not be required to transmit information that identifies executive branch budget decision items.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.10.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Duties to governor-elect.</span></h2>
          <p>Immediately after the election of a new governor, the commissioner shall report the budget estimates and make available to the governor-elect all department information, staff, and facilities relating to the budget.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16A.10.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">(<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1940A/cite/3">53-18m</a>) <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1939/0/431/#laws.3.14.0">1939 c 431 art 3 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/455/#laws.0.72.0">1977 c 455 s 72</a>,73; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/628/#laws.2.1.0">1984 c 628 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.1.59.0">1989 c 335 art 1 s 59</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/192/#laws.0.50.0">1993 c 192 s 50</a>,51; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/202/#laws.2.12.0">1997 c 202 art 2 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/366/#laws.0.21.0">1998 c 366 s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/10/#laws.2.21.0">1Sp2001 c 10 art 2 s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/112/#laws.1.3.0">2003 c 112 art 1 s 3</a>,4; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/318/#laws.1.2.0">2008 c 318 art 1 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/134/#laws.0.17.0">2013 c 134 s 17</a>,18; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/142/#laws.3.15.0">2013 c 142 art 3 s 15</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16A.101">
        <h1 class="shn">16A.101 SERVICE CONTRACTS; EXPENDITURES.</h1>
        <p>The state accounting system must list expenditures for professional and technical service contracts, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16C.08#stat.16C.08.1">16C.08, subdivision 1</a>, as a separate category. No other expenditures may be included in this category.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16A.101.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/254/#laws.1.44.0">1995 c 254 art 1 s 44</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/386/#laws.2.11.0">1998 c 386 art 2 s 11</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16A.102">
        <h1 class="shn">16A.102 BUDGETING REVENUES RELATIVE TO PERSONAL INCOME.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.102.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/148/#laws.2.84.0">2007 c 148 art 2 s 84</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.102.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/148/#laws.2.84.0">2007 c 148 art 2 s 84</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.102.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/148/#laws.2.84.0">2007 c 148 art 2 s 84</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.102.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Reporting information.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">At the time of a state revenue and expenditure forecast as specified in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.103#stat.16A.103.1">16A.103, subdivision 1</a>, and after the completion of a legislative session, the Department of Management and Budget must report on revenue relative to personal income. The information must specify (1) the share of personal income to be collected in taxes and other revenues to pay for state and local government services and (2) the division of that revenue between state and local government revenues.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16A.102.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/587/#laws.7.1.0">1994 c 587 art 7 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/389/#laws.16.8.0">1998 c 389 art 16 s 8</a>,9; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/250/#laws.1.45.0">1999 c 250 art 1 s 45</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/1/#laws.2.31.0">1Sp2003 c 1 art 2 s 31</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/284/#laws.2.4.0">2004 c 284 art 2 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/148/#laws.2.11.0">2007 c 148 art 2 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.109.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 109</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16A.103">
        <h1 class="shn">16A.103 FORECASTS OF REVENUE AND EXPENDITURES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.103.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">State revenue and expenditures.</span></h2>
          <p>In February and November each year, the commissioner shall prepare a forecast of state revenue and expenditures. The November forecast must be delivered to the legislature and governor no later than December 6. The February forecast must be delivered to the legislature and governor by the end of February. Forecasts must be delivered to the legislature and governor on the same day. If requested by the Legislative Commission on Planning and Fiscal Policy, delivery to the legislature must include a presentation to the commission.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.103.1a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1a.<span class="headnote">Forecast parameters.</span></h2>
          <p>The forecast must assume the continuation of current laws and reasonable estimates of projected growth in the national and state economies and affected populations. Revenue must be estimated for all sources provided for in current law. Expenditures must be estimated for all obligations imposed by law and those projected to occur as a result of inflation and variables outside the control of the legislature.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.103.1b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1b.<span class="headnote">Forecast variable.</span></h2>
          <p>In determining the rate of inflation, the application of inflation, the amount of state bonding as it affects debt service, the calculation of investment income, and the other variables to be included in the expenditure part of the forecast, the commissioner must consult with the chairs and lead minority members of the senate Finance Committee and the house of representatives Ways and Means Committee, and legislative fiscal staff. This consultation must occur at least three weeks before the forecast is to be released. No later than two weeks prior to the release of the forecast, the commissioner must inform the chairs and lead minority members of the senate Finance Committee and the house of representatives Ways and Means Committee, and legislative fiscal staff of any changes in these variables from the previous forecast.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.103.1c">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1c.<span class="headnote">Expenditure data.</span></h2>
          <p>State agencies must submit any revisions in expenditure data the commissioner determines necessary for the forecast to the commissioner at least four weeks prior to the release of the forecast. The information submitted by state agencies and any modifications to that information made by the commissioner must be made available to legislative fiscal staff no later than three weeks prior to the release of the forecast.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.103.1d">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1d.<span class="headnote">Revenue data.</span></h2>
          <p>On a monthly basis, the commissioner must provide legislative fiscal staff with an update of the previous month's state revenues no later than 12 days after the end of that month.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.103.1e">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1e.<span class="headnote">Economic information.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The commissioner must review economic information including economic forecasts with legislative fiscal staff no later than two weeks before the forecast is released. The commissioner must invite the chairs and lead minority members of the senate Finance Committee and the house of representatives Ways and Means Committee, and legislative fiscal staff to attend any meetings held with outside economic advisors. The commissioner must provide legislative fiscal staff with monthly economic forecast information received from outside sources.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.103.1f">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1f.<span class="headnote">Personal income.</span></h2>
          <p>In addition, the commissioner shall forecast Minnesota personal income for each of the years covered by the forecast and include these estimates in the forecast documents.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.103.1g">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1g.<span class="headnote">Period to be forecast.</span></h2>
          <p>A forecast prepared during the first fiscal year of a biennium must cover that biennium and the next biennium. A forecast prepared during the second fiscal year of a biennium must cover that biennium and the next two bienniums.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.103.1h">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1h.<span class="headnote">Revenue uncertainty information.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall report to the legislature within 14 days of a forecast under subdivision 1 on uncertainty in Minnesota's general fund revenue projections. The report shall present information on: (1) the estimated range of forecast error for revenues; and (2) the data and methods used to construct those measurements.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.103.1i">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1i.<span class="headnote">Budget close report.</span></h2>
          <p>By October 15 of each odd-numbered year, the commissioner shall prepare a detailed fund balance analysis of the general fund for the previous biennium. The analysis shall include a comparison to the most recent publicly available fund balance analysis of the general fund. The commissioner shall provide this analysis to the chairs and ranking minority members of the house of representatives Ways and Means Committee and the senate Finance Committee, and shall post the analysis on the agency's website.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.103.1j">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1j.<span class="headnote">Federal reimbursement for administrative costs.</span></h2>
          <p>In preparing the forecast of state revenues and expenditures under subdivision 1, the commissioner must include estimates of the amount of federal reimbursement for administrative costs for the Department of Human Services and the Department of Children, Youth, and Families in the forecast as an expenditure reduction. The amount included under this subdivision must conform with generally accepted accounting principles.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.103.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Local revenue.</span></h2>
