      <h1 class="fgh">STATE AGENCIES</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16A.126">
        <h1 class="shn">16A.126 REVOLVING FUND BILLING.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.126.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Set rates.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall approve the rates an agency must pay to a revolving fund for services. Funds subject to this subdivision include, but are not limited to, the revolving funds established in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.46">14.46</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.53">14.53</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16B.2975">16B.2975</a>, subdivision 4; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16B.48">16B.48</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16B.54">16B.54</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16B.58">16B.58</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16B.85">16B.85</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16E.14">16E.14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/43A.55">43A.55</a>; and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.591">176.591</a>; the fund established in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/43A.30">43A.30</a>; and the account established in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.1286">16A.1286</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.126.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Immediate needs.</span></h2>
          <p>To reduce reserves for unforeseen needs, and so reduce these rates, the commissioner may transfer money from the general fund to a revolving fund. Before doing so, the commissioner must decide there is not enough money in the revolving fund for an immediate, necessary expenditure. The amount necessary to make the transfer is appropriated from the general fund to the commissioner of management and budget. The commissioner shall report the amount and purpose of the transfer to the chair of the committee or division in the senate and house of representatives with primary jurisdiction over the budget of the Department of Management and Budget.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.126.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Repayment schedules.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall make schedules for repayment to the general fund of the transferred money. A schedule to repay money used to buy equipment may extend over the equipment's useful life. Otherwise, a schedule may not extend beyond five years. The repayment must include interest at a rate comparable to the rate earned by the state on invested commissioner of management and budget's cash, as determined monthly by the commissioner. An amount necessary to pay the interest is appropriated from the revolving fund to which the transfer was made.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16A.126.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/231/#laws.0.5.0">1976 c 231 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/410/#laws.0.5.0">1977 c 410 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1979/0/333/#laws.0.72.0">1979 c 333 s 72</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/614/#laws.0.55.0">1980 c 614 s 55</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/628/#laws.2.1.0">1984 c 628 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/275/#laws.0.3.0">1987 c 275 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/250/#laws.1.48.0">1999 c 250 art 1 s 48</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/488/#laws.12.11.0">2000 c 488 art 12 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/112/#laws.2.50.0">2003 c 112 art 2 s 50</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.45.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 45</a>,109; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/382/#laws.0.6.0">2010 c 382 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/196/#laws.2.15.0">2014 c 196 art 2 s 15</a>; art 3 s 1; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/21/#laws.1.5.0">2015 c 21 art 1 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/62/#laws.2.55.0">2023 c 62 art 2 s 55</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16A.127">
        <h1 class="shn">16A.127 INDIRECT COSTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.127.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Statewide and agency indirect costs.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) As used in this section, "statewide indirect costs" means all general fund expenditures made by any state agency attributable to providing general support services to any other state agency.</p>
          <p>(b) As used in this section, "agency indirect costs" means all general support costs within any agency that cannot be directly charged to any agency program.</p>
          <p>(c) For purposes of this section, "agency" means any entity receiving general support services.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.127.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Statewide plan.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall annually prepare a plan identifying the sources and amounts of each agency's statewide indirect costs for the current fiscal year. The commissioner shall submit the plan to the cognizant federal agency for approval, and provide copies to the governor and the legislature.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.127.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">General reimbursement.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Unless indirect cost recoveries are specifically appropriated in law, agencies are obligated to reimburse the general fund for all statewide indirect costs, and that portion of agency indirect costs attributable to recoveries of general fund expenditures. However, the commissioner may, for reasons of sound financial management, waive the reimbursement under this subdivision for certain nongeneral fund activities.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner shall record the reimbursement to the general fund of the statewide and agency indirect costs attributable to an agency's nongeneral fund activities for the fiscal year. All nonfederal agency indirect cost receipts are appropriated to the agency to pay administrative expenses, unless they are determined to be a reimbursement of general fund expenditures.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.127.3a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3a.<span class="headnote">Appropriation.</span></h2>
          <p>There is annually appropriated from all direct appropriated nongeneral funds an amount sufficient to reimburse the general fund for both statewide indirect costs, and any agency indirect costs attributable to general fund expenditures.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.127.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Federal proposals.</span></h2>
          <p>Agency applications for federal money shall include necessary submissions to recover both statewide and agency indirect costs. An agency indirect cost plan is unnecessary if the commissioner determines that the costs incurred in preparing and maintaining it exceed the benefit received by the state. If less than the entire agency proposal is federally approved, the commissioner may accept reimbursement of less than all of the federal receipts. If no federal funds are approved for indirect costs, the agency must document that fact to the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.127.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Federal reimbursement.</span></h2>
          <p>Agencies shall reimburse the general fund for all federal money received as a recovery of statewide indirect costs. All federal agency indirect cost receipts are appropriated to the agency to pay administrative expenses, unless they are determined to be a reimbursement of general fund expenditures.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.127.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Required information.</span></h2>
          <p>Agencies must supply the information required by the commissioner, as needed, to carry out the provisions of this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.127.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Audit fees.</span></h2>
          <p>The legislative auditor may recommend waiver, and the Legislative Audit Commission may waive all or part of a fee for an audit. A state audited agency whose funds are not administered by the commissioner of management and budget must transfer to the general fund the amount of the cost of the audit attributable to the agency's nongeneral fund receipts.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.127.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Exemptions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) No statewide or agency indirect cost liability shall be accrued to any program, appropriation, or account that is specifically exempted from the liability in federal or state law, or if the commissioner determines the funds to be held in trust, or to be a pass-through, workshop, or seminar account. Accounts receiving proceeds from bond issues and general fund accounts are also exempt from this section.</p>
          <p>(b) Except for the costs of the legislative auditor to conduct financial audits of federal funds, this section does not apply to the Board of Trustees of the Minnesota State Colleges and Universities. Receipts attributable to financial audits conducted by the legislative auditor of federal funds administered by the board shall be deposited in the general fund.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.127.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Provision for natural resources.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The Department of Natural Resources is exempt from recovering agency indirect costs except where federal funds are involved.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner of natural resources need not bill the federal government, other states, or Canadian provinces for the indirect costs of providing emergency firefighting services, and need not reimburse the general fund for those indirect costs if the waiver is reciprocated.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16A.127.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/231/#laws.0.6.0">1976 c 231 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/301/#laws.0.89.0">1983 c 301 s 89</a>,90; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/628/#laws.2.1.0">1984 c 628 art 2 s 1</a>; art 6 s 1; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/654/#laws.2.57.0">1984 c 654 art 2 s 57</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/13/#laws.0.97.0">1Sp1985 c 13 s 97</a>-100; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/264/#laws.0.1.0">1987 c 264 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/275/#laws.0.4.0">1987 c 275 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/404/#laws.0.76.0">1987 c 404 s 76</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/375/#laws.0.3.0">1990 c 375 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/1/2/#laws.3.2.0">1Sp1993 c 2 art 3 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/632/#laws.3.26.0">1994 c 632 art 3 s 26</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/254/#laws.1.46.0">1995 c 254 art 1 s 46</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/395/#laws.0.18.0">1996 c 395 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/112/#laws.1.5.0">2003 c 112 art 1 s 5</a>; art 2 s 50; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.109.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 109</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/134/#laws.0.19.0">2013 c 134 s 19</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.16A.128"><b>16A.128</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/192/#laws.0.110.0">1993 c 192 s 110</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.16A.1281"><b>16A.1281</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/192/#laws.0.110.0">1993 c 192 s 110</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16A.1283">
        <h1 class="shn">16A.1283 LEGISLATIVE APPROVAL REQUIRED FOR FEES.</h1>
        <p>(a) Notwithstanding any law to the contrary, an executive branch state agency may not impose a new fee or increase an existing fee unless the new fee or increase is approved by law. For purposes of this section, a fee is any charge for goods, services, regulation, or licensure, and, notwithstanding paragraph (b), clause (3), includes charges for admission to or for use of public facilities owned by the state.</p>
        <p>(b) This section does not apply to:</p>
        <p>(1) charges billed within or between state agencies, or billed to federal agencies;</p>
        <p>(2) the Minnesota State Colleges and Universities system;</p>
        <p>(3) charges for goods and services provided for the direct and primary use of a private individual, business, or other entity;</p>
        <p>(4) charges that authorize use of state-owned lands and minerals administered by the commissioner of natural resources by the issuance of leases, easements, cooperative farming agreements, and land and water crossing licenses and charges for sales of state-owned lands administered by the commissioner of natural resources; or</p>
        <p>(5) state park fees and charges established by commissioner's order.</p>
        <p>(c) An executive branch agency may reduce a fee that was set by rule before July 1, 2001, without legislative approval. Chapter 14 does not apply to fee reductions under this paragraph.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16A.1283.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/250/#laws.1.49.0">1999 c 250 art 1 s 49</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/206/#laws.0.1.0">2001 c 206 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/28/#laws.1.1.0">2003 c 28 art 1 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16A.1285">
        <h1 class="shn">16A.1285 DEPARTMENTAL EARNINGS; CHARGES LEVIED BY AGENCY.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.1285.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>In this section, "departmental earnings" means any charge for goods and services and any regulatory, licensure, or other similar charges levied by any state agency and paid by individuals, businesses, or other nonstate entities. This definition must not be construed to include general taxes collected by a state agency or charges for services provided by one state agency to another state agency.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.1285.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Policy.</span></h2>
          <p>Unless otherwise provided by law, specific charges falling within definitions stipulated in subdivision 1 must be set at a level that neither significantly over recovers nor under recovers costs, including overhead costs, involved in providing the services.</p>
          <p>Unless specifically provided otherwise in statute, in setting, adjusting, or authorizing charges that in whole or in part recover previously unrecovered costs, recovery is limited to those unrecovered costs incurred during the two fiscal years immediately preceding the setting, adjustment, or authorization.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.1285.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Duties of commissioner of management and budget.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of management and budget shall classify, monitor, analyze, and report all departmental earnings that fall within the definition established in subdivision 1. Specifically, the commissioner shall:</p>
          <p>(1) establish and maintain a classification system that clearly defines and distinguishes categories and types of departmental earnings and takes into account the purpose of the various earnings types and the extent to which various earnings types serve a public or private interest;</p>
          <p>(2) prepare a biennial report that documents collection costs, purposes, and yields of all departmental earnings, the report to be submitted to the legislature on or before the fourth Tuesday in January in each odd-numbered year and to include estimated data for the year in which the report is prepared, actual data for the two years immediately before, and estimates for the two years immediately following; and</p>
          <p>(3) prepare and maintain a detailed directory of all departmental earnings.</p>
          <p class="bl">In a year following the election of a governor who had not been governor the previous year, the report required by clause (2) must be submitted by the third Tuesday in February.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.1285.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/250/#laws.1.115.0">1999 c 250 art 1 s 115</a> para (d)]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.1285.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/250/#laws.1.115.0">1999 c 250 art 1 s 115</a> para (d)]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16A.1285.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/192/#laws.0.56.0">1993 c 192 s 56</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/233/#laws.2.32.0">1995 c 233 art 2 s 32</a>-34; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/202/#laws.2.17.0">1997 c 202 art 2 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/1/#laws.2.33.0">1Sp2003 c 1 art 2 s 33</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.109.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 109</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">COMMISSIONER DUTIES; AUTHORITY</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16A.1286">
        <h1 class="shn">16A.1286 STATEWIDE SYSTEMS ACCOUNT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.1286.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Continuation.</span></h2>
          <p>The statewide systems account is a separate account in the special revenue fund. All money resulting from billings for statewide systems services must be deposited in the account. For the purposes of this section, statewide systems includes the state accounting system, payroll system, human resources systems, procurement system, and related information access systems.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.1286.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Billing procedures.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The commissioner may bill each fiscal year for statewide systems services provided to state agencies in the executive, legislative, and judicial branches, the Minnesota State Colleges and Universities, and other entities. Each entity shall be billed based on that entity's usage of the statewide systems. Each agency shall transfer from agency operating appropriations to the statewide systems account the amount billed by the commissioner. The commissioner shall develop billing policies and procedures.</p>
          <p class="see_note">[See Note.]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.1286.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Appropriation.</span></h2>
          <p>Money transferred into the account is appropriated to the commissioner to pay for statewide systems services.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.1286.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/379/#laws.1.114.0">2002 c 379 art 1 s 114</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.1286.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/379/#laws.1.114.0">2002 c 379 art 1 s 114</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16A.1286.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/10/#laws.2.22.0">1Sp2001 c 10 art 2 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/156/#laws.2.14.0">2005 c 156 art 2 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/148/#laws.2.13.0">2007 c 148 art 2 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/204/#laws.0.42.0">2008 c 204 s 42</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.109.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 109</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/10/#laws.3.24.0">1Sp2011 c 10 art 3 s 24</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/62/#laws.2.56.0">2023 c 62 art 2 s 56</a></p>
      </div>
      <p><b>NOTE: </b>The amendment to subdivision 2 by Laws 2023, chapter 62, article 2, section 56, is effective July 1, 2025. Laws 2023, chapter 62, article 2, section 56, the effective date.</p>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16A.129">
        <h1 class="shn">16A.129 OTHER COMMISSIONER POWERS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.129.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">List of salaries.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may require a list of the employees of an agency, and that their salaries conform with the scale of compensation established by law.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.129.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Classified budget, accounts.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may classify expenditures and revenue for budget making and accounting.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.129.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Cash advances.</span></h2>
          <p>When the operations of any account would be impeded by projected cash deficiencies resulting from delays in the receipt of grants, dedicated income, or other similar receivables, and when the deficiencies would be corrected within the budget period involved, the commissioner of management and budget may use fund level cash reserves to meet cash demands. If funds are transferred from the general fund to meet cash flow needs, the cash flow transfers must be returned to the general fund as soon as sufficient cash balances are available in the account to which the transfer was made. Any interest earned on general fund cash flow transfers accrues to the general fund and not to the accounts or funds to which the transfer was made. The commissioner may advance general fund cash reserves to nongeneral fund accounts where the receipts from other governmental units cannot be collected within the budget period.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16A.129.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">(<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1940A/cite/3">53-7</a>) <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1925/0/426/#laws.3.4.0">1925 c 426 art 3 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1939/0/441/#laws.0.39.0">1939 c 441 s 39</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/492/#laws.0.14.0">1973 c 492 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/507/#laws.0.45.0">1973 c 507 s 45</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/2/#laws.0.5.0">1976 c 2 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/231/#laws.0.7.0">1976 c 231 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/347/#laws.0.8.0">1977 c 347 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/674/#laws.0.5.0">1978 c 674 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/628/#laws.2.1.0">1984 c 628 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/192/#laws.0.57.0">1993 c 192 s 57</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/254/#laws.1.47.0">1995 c 254 art 1 s 47</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/202/#laws.2.18.0">1997 c 202 art 2 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/250/#laws.1.50.0">1999 c 250 art 1 s 50</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/464/#laws.1.1.0">2000 c 464 art 1 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/488/#laws.12.12.0">2000 c 488 art 12 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/112/#laws.1.6.0">2003 c 112 art 1 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.109.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 109</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16A.13">
        <h1 class="shn">16A.13 FEDERAL TAX WITHHOLDING.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.13.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Custodian; bond.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of management and budget is the custodian of all money deposited with the commissioner of management and budget for federal tax withheld from the pay of any officer or employee of the state of Minnesota. The commissioner of management and budget's bond to the state shall cover the liability for the custodian's acts. The deposits are subject to laws on keeping and paying out state money.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.13.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Commissioner as federal agent.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may cooperate with and act as agent for the United States of America in collecting federal tax from the pay of employees.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.13.2a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2a.<span class="headnote">Procedure.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall see that the deduction for the withheld tax is made from an employee's pay on the payroll abstract. The commissioner shall approve one payment to the commissioner for the total amount deducted on the abstract. Deductions from the pay of an employee paid direct by an agency shall be made by the employee's payroll authority. A later deduction must correct an error made on an earlier deduction. The paying authority shall see that a payment for the deductions is promptly sent to the commissioner. The commissioner shall deposit the amount of the payment to the credit of the proper federal authority or other person authorized by federal law to receive it.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.13.2b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2b.<span class="headnote">Appropriation.</span></h2>
          <p>There is appropriated the amount necessary to discharge the state's obligation under federal law requiring the deductions from pay in this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.13.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Reports; payments.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall report as required by federal law on the deductions made under this section and see that the deducted money is paid out as required.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.13.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Employees to provide information.</span></h2>
          <p>An employee shall prepare and send to the commissioner the information and forms the commissioner requires under this section.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16A.13.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1943/0/1/#laws.0.1.0">1943 c 1 s 1</a>-4; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/492/#laws.0.14.0">1973 c 492 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/628/#laws.2.1.0">1984 c 628 art 2 s 1</a>; art 6 s 1; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/654/#laws.2.58.0">1984 c 654 art 2 s 58</a>,59; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; 2003 s 112 art 2 s 26; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.109.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 109</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/10/#laws.3.4.0">1Sp2019 c 10 art 3 s 4</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">EMPLOYEES</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16A.131">
        <h1 class="shn">16A.131 DEDUCTIONS FOR UNITED STATES SECURITIES, TRANSIT CARDS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.131.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/112/#laws.1.19.0">2003 c 112 art 1 s 19</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.131.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Transit cards.</span></h2>
          <p>An employee may direct the commissioner, in writing, to deduct a stated amount from the employee's pay to buy mass transit ridership cards.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16A.131.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1951/0/678/#laws.0.1.0">1951 c 678 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/614/#laws.0.56.0">1980 c 614 s 56</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/544/#laws.0.89.0">1984 c 544 s 89</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/628/#laws.2.1.0">1984 c 628 art 2 s 1</a>; art 6 s 1; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/654/#laws.2.60.0">1984 c 654 art 2 s 60</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/158/#laws.1.10.0">2016 c 158 art 1 s 10</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.16A.132"><b>16A.132</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/628/#laws.2.4.0">1984 c 628 art 2 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/654/#laws.2.155.0">1984 c 654 art 2 s 155</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16A.133">
        <h1 class="shn">16A.133 CREDIT UNION, PARKING, OTHER DEDUCTIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.133.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Payroll direct deposit and deductions.</span></h2>
          <p>An agency head in the executive, judicial, and legislative branch shall, upon written request signed by an employee, directly deposit all or part of an employee's pay to those credit unions or financial institutions, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/47.015">47.015</a>, designated by the employee.</p>
          <p>An agency head must, upon written request of an employee, deduct from the pay of the employee a requested amount to be paid to the Minnesota Benefit Association, or to any organizations contemplated by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/179A.06">179A.06</a>, of which the employee is a member.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.133.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Parking, and the like.</span></h2>
          <p>With the written consent of an employee, an agency head shall deduct from the employee's pay the amount needed to pay for services or facilities supplied under law to the employee by the state. Food and housing, garage and parking facilities, and other facilities and services may be paid for in this way.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.133.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.1.270.0">1989 c 335 art 1 s 270</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16A.133.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1941/0/464/#laws.0.1.0">1941 c 464 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1955/0/108/#laws.0.1.0">1955 c 108 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/130/#laws.0.1.0">1969 c 130 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1971/0/841/#laws.0.1.0">1971 c 841 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/35/#laws.0.3.0">1973 c 35 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/607/#laws.19.1.0">1980 c 607 art 19 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/628/#laws.2.2.0">1984 c 628 art 2 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/248/#laws.0.7.0">1985 c 248 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/337/#laws.0.1.0">1987 c 337 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.1.61.0">1989 c 335 art 1 s 61</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/594/#laws.1.44.0">1990 c 594 art 1 s 44</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/238/#laws.4.1.0">1991 c 238 art 4 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/112/#laws.1.7.0">2003 c 112 art 1 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.46.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 46</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16A.1335">
        <h1 class="shn">16A.1335 EMPLOYEE SALARIES AND BENEFITS IN EVENT OF STATE GOVERNMENT SHUTDOWN.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.1335.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definition.</span></h2>
          <p>As used in this section, "government shutdown" means that, as of July 1 of an odd-numbered year, legislation appropriating money for the general operations of (1) an executive agency, (2) an office or department of the legislature, including each house of the legislature and the Legislative Coordinating Commission, or (3) a judicial branch agency or department, including a court, has not been enacted for the biennium beginning July 1 of that year.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.1335.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Payment required.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.17#stat.16A.17.8">16A.17, subdivision 8</a>, state employees must be provided payment for lost salary and benefits resulting from their absence from work during a government shutdown. An employee is eligible for a payment under this section only upon the employee's return to work.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.1335.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Appropriation; limitation.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) In the event of a government shutdown, the amount necessary to pay the salary and benefits of employees of any impacted agency, office, or department is appropriated beginning on that July 1 to that agency, office, or department. The appropriation is made from the fund or funds from which an appropriation was made in the previous fiscal year for salary and benefits paid to each affected employee.</p>
          <p>(b) Amounts appropriated under this subdivision may not exceed the amount or amounts appropriated for general operations of the affected agency, office, or department in the previous fiscal year.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.1335.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Certification of amount for employees in the legislative and judicial branches.</span></h2>
          <p>By June 25 of an odd-numbered year, if a government shutdown appears imminent, the director of the Legislative Coordinating Commission, the chief clerk of the house of representatives, the secretary of the senate, and the chief clerk of the supreme court must each certify to the commissioner of management and budget the amount needed for salaries and benefits for each fiscal year of the next biennium, and the commissioner of management and budget shall make the certified amount available on July 1 of that year or on another schedule that permits payment of all salary and benefit obligations required by this section in a timely manner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.1335.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Subsequent appropriations.</span></h2>
          <p>A subsequent appropriation to the agency, office, or department for regular operations for a biennium in which this section has been applied may only supersede and replace the appropriation provided by subdivision 3 by express reference to this section.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16A.1335.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/53/#laws.11.2.0">2023 c 53 art 11 s 2</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">STATE OFFICIALS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16A.134">
        <h1 class="shn">16A.134 CHARITABLE ORGANIZATIONS PAYROLL DEDUCTIONS.</h1>
        <p>An employee's contribution to a registered combined charitable organization defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/43A.50">43A.50</a> may be deducted from the employee's pay. On the employee's written request, the commissioner shall deduct a requested amount from the pay of the employee for each pay period. The commissioner shall issue a payment in that amount to the specified organization.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16A.134.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1965/0/766/#laws.0.1.0">1965 c 766 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/492/#laws.0.14.0">1973 c 492 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/628/#laws.2.3.0">1984 c 628 art 2 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/101/#laws.0.1.0">2007 c 101 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/10/#laws.3.40.0">1Sp2019 c 10 art 3 s 40</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16A.138">
        <h1 class="shn">16A.138 OFFICIALS NOT TO EXCEED APPROPRIATION.</h1>
        <p>When there has been an appropriation for any purpose it shall be unlawful for any state board or official to incur indebtedness on behalf of the board, the official, or the state in excess of the appropriation made for such purpose. It is hereby made unlawful for any state board or official to incur any indebtedness in behalf of the board, the official, or the state of any nature until after an appropriation therefor has been made by the legislature. Any official violating these provisions shall be guilty of a misdemeanor and the governor is hereby authorized and empowered to remove any such official from office.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16A.138.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">(<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1940A/cite/4">125</a>) <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1907/0/272/#laws.0.2.0">1907 c 272 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1919/1/35/#laws.0.11.0">Ex1919 c 35 s 11</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16A.139">
        <h1 class="shn">16A.139 MISAPPROPRIATION OF MONEY.</h1>
        <p>(a) No official or head of any state department in the executive, legislative, or judicial branches, or any employee of a state department in those branches, may intentionally use money appropriated by law, or fees collected knowing that the use is for a purpose other than the purpose for which the money was appropriated. Unless a greater penalty is specified elsewhere in law, a person who violates this paragraph is guilty of a gross misdemeanor.</p>
        <p>(b) A violation of paragraph (a) by a head of a department, or any state official, is cause for immediate removal of the official or head of a state department from the position held with the government of this state. A criminal conviction under paragraph (a) is not a prerequisite for removal. This paragraph does not apply to a judge, a constitutional officer, or a legislator, except as potential grounds for expulsion, impeachment, or recall in the manner specified in article IV, section 7, and article VIII of the Minnesota Constitution.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16A.139.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">(<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1940A/cite/4">125-14</a>) <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1937/0/457/#laws.0.36.0">1937 c 457 s 36</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1979/0/333/#laws.0.60.0">1979 c 333 s 60</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.47.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 47</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">ACCOUNTING</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16A.14">
        <h1 class="shn">16A.14 ALLOTMENT AND ENCUMBRANCE SYSTEM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.14.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Less than fiscal year.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may set an allotment period shorter than and not extending beyond the fiscal year.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.14.1a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1a.<span class="headnote">Permanent improvements.</span></h2>
          <p>Subdivision 1 does not apply for allotments of appropriations for permanent improvements, including acquisition of real property.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.14.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Application.</span></h2>
          <p>The allotment and encumbrance system applies to all appropriations and funds except as provided in subdivisions 2a, 2b, and 2c.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.14.2a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2a.<span class="headnote">Exceptions.</span></h2>
          <p>The allotment and encumbrance system does not apply to:</p>
          <p>(1) appropriations for the courts or the legislature;</p>
          <p>(2) payment of unemployment benefits; and</p>
          <p>(3) transactions within the defined contribution funds administered by the Minnesota State Retirement System.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.14.2b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2b.<span class="headnote">Impractical allotments.</span></h2>
          <p>With permanent improvement contracts and transactions for the acquisition of real estate, equipment, repair, rehabilitation, appurtenances or utility systems to be used for public purposes, the commissioner may do away with periodic allotments as impractical and make rules to ensure the proper application and encumbering of funds.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.14.2c">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2c.<span class="headnote">Contingent funds.</span></h2>
          <p>Contingent appropriations for the governor and the attorney general are not subject to allotment. They are subject to the prescriptions in this chapter relating to spending and encumbering of funds.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.14.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Spending plan.</span></h2>
          <p>An appropriation to an agency may not be made available for spending in the next allotment period until the agency has met all the requirements related to the policies and procedures of the Minnesota accounting and procurement system. A spending plan shall be submitted by July 31 to the commissioner on the commissioner's form. The spending plan must certify that: the amount required for each activity is accurate and is consistent with legislative intent; revenue estimates are reasonable; and the plan is structurally balanced, with all legal restrictions on spending having been met for the purpose for which money is to be spent.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.14.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Approval.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall approve the estimated amount for expenditure if the spending plan is within the amount and purpose of the appropriation. In doing so, the commissioner must keep in mind the probable needs of the agency for the rest of the term of the appropriation, and whether there is a need for the appropriation in the next allotment period. Otherwise the commissioner shall modify the spending plan and the allotment to conform with the appropriation and the future needs of the agency. The commissioner shall act promptly on a spending plan. The commissioner shall notify an agency of its allotments at least five days before an allotment period. Allotments to an agency for an appropriation term may not exceed the amount appropriated for that term.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.14.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Modification.</span></h2>
          <p>After approval, the commissioner may modify a spending plan for cause. An agency may apply for and must be notified of the modification. The modification may not result in a deficit or an undue reduction of funds to meet future agency needs.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16A.14.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/166/#laws.0.7.0">1976 c 166 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/231/#laws.0.8.0">1976 c 231 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/628/#laws.2.1.0">1984 c 628 art 2 s 1</a>; art 6 s 1; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/654/#laws.2.61.0">1984 c 654 art 2 s 61</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/488/#laws.0.8.0">1994 c 488 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/107/#laws.0.66.0">1999 c 107 s 66</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/343/#laws.0.4.0">2000 c 343 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/112/#laws.1.8.0">2003 c 112 art 1 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2018/0/211/#laws.9.3.0">2018 c 211 art 9 s 3</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16A.15">
        <h1 class="shn">16A.15 ACCOUNTING SYSTEM; ALLOTMENT AND ENCUMBRANCE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.15.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.</h2>
          <p>[Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.152">16A.152, subd 4</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.15.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Accounting system.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall keep an accounting system in the department's office showing by fund and item:</p>
          <p>(1) the amounts appropriated for and the estimated revenue of the agency;</p>
          <p>(2) the amount allotted and available for expenditure;</p>
          <p>(3) the amount of expenditures or obligations authorized to be incurred;</p>
          <p>(4) the actual receipts and disbursements;</p>
          <p>(5) actual balances on hand; and</p>
          <p>(6) the unencumbered balances after deduction of all actual and authorized expenditures.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.15.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Allotment and encumbrance.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A payment may not be made without prior obligation. An obligation may not be incurred against any fund, allotment, or appropriation unless the commissioner has certified a sufficient unencumbered balance or the accounting system shows sufficient allotment or encumbrance balance in the fund, allotment, or appropriation to meet it. The commissioner shall determine when the accounting system may be used to incur obligations without the commissioner's certification of a sufficient unencumbered balance. An expenditure or obligation authorized or incurred in violation of this chapter is invalid and ineligible for payment until made valid. A payment made in violation of this chapter is illegal. An employee authorizing or making the payment, or taking part in it, and a person receiving any part of the payment, are jointly and severally liable to the state for the amount paid or received. If an employee knowingly incurs an obligation or authorizes or makes an expenditure in violation of this chapter or takes part in the violation, the violation is just cause for the employee's removal by the appointing authority or by the governor if an appointing authority other than the governor fails to do so. In the latter case, the governor shall give notice of the violation and an opportunity to be heard on it to the employee and to the appointing authority. A claim presented against an appropriation without prior allotment or encumbrance may be made valid on investigation, review, and approval by the agency head in accordance with the commissioner's policy, if the services, materials, or supplies to be paid for were actually furnished in good faith without collusion and without intent to defraud. The commissioner may then pay the claim just as properly allotted and encumbered claims are paid.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner may approve payment for materials and supplies in excess of the obligation amount when increases are authorized by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16C.03#stat.16C.03.3">16C.03, subdivision 3</a>.</p>
          <p>(c) To minimize potential construction delay claims, an agency with a project funded by a building appropriation may allow a consultant or contractor to proceed with supplemental work within the limits of the appropriation before money is encumbered. Under this circumstance, the agency may requisition funds and allow consultants or contractors to expeditiously proceed with services or a construction sequence. While the consultant or contractor is proceeding, the agency shall immediately act to encumber the required funds.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.15.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Periodic allotment.</span></h2>
          <p>In the case of appropriations made for permanent improvements, including acquisition of real property, which appropriations do not lapse until the purposes of the appropriations are accomplished or abandoned, the commissioner may dispense with periodic allotments and shall make rules to ensure the proper application and encumbrance of funds.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.15.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.</h2>
          <p>[Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.152">16A.152, subd 6</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.15.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.</h2>
          <p>[Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.152#stat.16A.152.1">16A.152, subdivision 1</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.15.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.</h2>
          <p>[Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.152">16A.152, subd 7</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16A.15.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/492/#laws.0.23.0">1973 c 492 s 23</a>-26; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/231/#laws.0.10.0">1976 c 231 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/793/#laws.0.47.0">1978 c 793 s 47</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/1/#laws.0.2.0">1981 c 1 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/1/5/#laws.0.1.0">1Sp1981 c 5 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/2/1/#laws.0.3.0">2Sp1981 c 1 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/3/1/#laws.1.1.0">3Sp1981 c 1 art 1 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/3/2/#laws.2.3.0">3Sp1981 c 2 art 2 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/342/#laws.18.1.0">1983 c 342 art 18 s 1</a>-3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/502/#laws.1.1.0">1984 c 502 art 1 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/544/#laws.0.89.0">1984 c 544 s 89</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/628/#laws.2.1.0">1984 c 628 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/14/#laws.18.1.0">1Sp1985 c 14 art 18 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/1/1/#laws.5.1.0">1Sp1986 c 1 art 5 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/268/#laws.18.1.0">1987 c 268 art 18 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/719/#laws.13.1.0">1988 c 719 art 13 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/1/1/#laws.15.1.0">1Sp1989 c 1 art 15 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/291/#laws.21.2.0">1991 c 291 art 21 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/511/#laws.9.1.0">1992 c 511 art 9 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/514/#laws.0.1.0">1992 c 514 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/192/#laws.0.58.0">1993 c 192 s 58</a>-60,111; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/375/#laws.17.1.0">1993 c 375 art 17 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/632/#laws.3.27.0">1994 c 632 art 3 s 27</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/202/#laws.2.19.0">1997 c 202 art 2 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/386/#laws.2.12.0">1998 c 386 art 2 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/10/#laws.3.5.0">1Sp2019 c 10 art 3 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/62/#laws.2.57.0">2023 c 62 art 2 s 57</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16A.151">
        <h1 class="shn">16A.151 PROCEEDS OF LITIGATION OR SETTLEMENT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.151.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">State funds; general fund.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) This subdivision applies, notwithstanding any law to the contrary, except as provided in subdivision 2.</p>
          <p>(b) A state official may not commence, pursue, or settle litigation, or settle a matter that could have resulted in litigation, in a manner that would result in money being distributed to a person or entity other than the state.</p>
          <p>(c) Money recovered by a state official in litigation or in settlement of a matter that could have resulted in litigation is state money and must be deposited in the general fund.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.151.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Exceptions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) If a state official litigates or settles a matter on behalf of specific injured persons or entities, this section does not prohibit distribution of money to the specific injured persons or entities on whose behalf the litigation or settlement efforts were initiated. If money recovered on behalf of injured persons or entities cannot reasonably be distributed to those persons or entities because they cannot readily be located or identified or because the cost of distributing the money would outweigh the benefit to the persons or entities, the money must be paid into the general fund.</p>
          <p>(b) Money recovered on behalf of a fund in the state treasury other than the general fund may be deposited in that fund.</p>
          <p>(c) This section does not prohibit a state official from distributing money to a person or entity other than the state in litigation or potential litigation in which the state is a defendant or potential defendant.</p>
          <p>(d) State agencies may accept funds as directed by a federal court for any restitution or monetary penalty under United States Code, title 18, section 3663(a)(3), or United States Code, title 18, section 3663A(a)(3). Funds received must be deposited in a special revenue account and are appropriated to the commissioner of the agency for the purpose as directed by the federal court.</p>
          <p>(e) Tobacco settlement revenues as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.98#stat.16A.98.1">16A.98, subdivision 1</a>, paragraph (t), may be deposited as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.98#stat.16A.98.12">16A.98, subdivision 12</a>.</p>
          <p>(f) Any money received by the state resulting from a settlement agreement or an assurance of discontinuance entered into by the attorney general of the state, or a court order in litigation brought by the attorney general of the state, on behalf of the state or a state agency, related to alleged violations of consumer fraud laws in the marketing, sale, or distribution of opioids in this state or other alleged illegal actions that contributed to the excessive use of opioids, must be deposited in the settlement account established in the opiate epidemic response fund under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256.043">256.043, subdivision </a>1. This paragraph does not apply to attorney fees and costs awarded to the state or the Attorney General's Office, to contract attorneys hired by the state or Attorney General's Office, or to other state agency attorneys.</p>
          <p>(g) Notwithstanding paragraph (f), if money is received from a settlement agreement or an assurance of discontinuance entered into by the attorney general of the state or a court order in litigation brought by the attorney general of the state on behalf of the state or a state agency against a consulting firm working for an opioid manufacturer or opioid wholesale drug distributor, the commissioner shall deposit any money received into the settlement account established within the opiate epidemic response fund under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256.042#stat.256.042.1">256.042, subdivision 1</a>. Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256.043">256.043, subdivision </a>3a, paragraph (a), any amount deposited into the settlement account in accordance with this paragraph shall be appropriated to the commissioner of human services to award as grants as specified by the opiate epidemic response advisory council in accordance with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256.043#stat.256.043.3a">256.043, subdivision 3a</a>, paragraph (e).</p>
          <p>(h) If the Minnesota Pollution Control Agency, through litigation or settlement of a matter that could have resulted in litigation, recovers $250,000 or more in a civil penalty from violations of a permit issued by the agency, then 40 percent of the money recovered must be distributed to the community health board, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145A.02">145A.02</a>, where the permitted facility is located. Within 30 days of a final court order in the litigation or the effective date of the settlement agreement, the commissioner of the Minnesota Pollution Control Agency must notify the applicable community health board that the litigation has concluded or a settlement has been reached. The commissioner must collect the money and transfer it to the applicable community health board. The community health board must meet directly with the residents potentially affected by the pollution that was the subject of the litigation or settlement to identify the residents' concerns and incorporate those concerns into a project that benefits the residents. The project must be implemented by the community health board and funded as directed in this paragraph. The community health board may recover the reasonable costs it incurs to administer this paragraph from the funds transferred to the board under this paragraph. This paragraph directs the transfer and use of money only and does not create a right of intervention in the litigation or settlement of the enforcement action for any person or entity. A supplemental environmental project funded as part of a settlement agreement is not part of a civil penalty and must not be included in calculating the amount of funds required to be distributed to a community health board under this paragraph. For the purposes of this paragraph, "supplemental environmental project" means a project that benefits the environment or public health that a regulated facility agrees to undertake, though not legally required to do so, as part of a settlement with respect to an enforcement action taken by the Minnesota Pollution Control Agency to resolve noncompliance.</p>
          <p>(i) A community health board receiving a transfer of funds under paragraph (h) must, no later than one year after receiving the funds, submit a report to the chairs and ranking minority members of the senate and house of representatives committees with primary jurisdiction over environment policy and natural resources that describes:</p>
          <p>(1) the process of community engagement employed to solicit community input regarding the use of the funds;</p>
          <p>(2) the purposes and activities for which the funds were used; and</p>
          <p>(3) an account of expenditures.</p>
          <p>(j) The commissioner of the Minnesota Pollution Control Agency must submit a report in September each even-numbered year, beginning in 2024, to the chairs and ranking minority members of the senate and house of representatives committees with primary jurisdiction over environmental policy and natural resources that includes:</p>
          <p>(1) the amount transferred under paragraph (h) to each community health board during the previous two years; and</p>
          <p>(2) any agency services provided to the community health board or community residents during the duration of the project funded by the transfer, and the cost of those agency services, for consideration by the legislature for future appropriations that address reimbursement of the amount of the transfers and the cost of services provided by the agency.</p>
          <p>(k) Any money received by the state resulting from a settlement agreement or an assurance of discontinuance entered into by the attorney general of the state, or a court order in litigation brought by the attorney general of the state on behalf of the state or a state agency related to alleged violations of consumer fraud laws in the marketing, sale, or distribution of electronic nicotine delivery systems in this state or other alleged illegal actions that contributed to the exacerbation of youth nicotine use, must be deposited in the tobacco use prevention account under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/144.398">144.398</a>. This paragraph does not apply to: (1) attorney fees and costs awarded or paid to the state or the Attorney General's Office; (2) contract attorneys hired by the state or Attorney General's Office; or (3) other state agency attorneys. The commissioner of management and budget must transfer to the tobacco use prevention account, any money subject to this paragraph that is received by the state before May 24, 2023.</p>
          <p class="see_note">[See Note.]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.151.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>For purposes of this section:</p>
          <p>(1) "litigation" includes civil, criminal, and administrative actions;</p>
          <p>(2) "money recovered" includes actual damages, punitive or exemplary damages, statutory damages, and civil and criminal penalties; and</p>
          <p>(3) "state official" means the attorney general, another constitutional officer, an agency, or an agency employee, acting in official capacity.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.151.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Supersede.</span></h2>
          <p>This section supersedes section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/8.31#stat.8.31.2c">8.31, subdivision 2c</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.151.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/156/#laws.2.52.0">2005 c 156 art 2 s 52</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16A.151.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/10/#laws.2.23.0">1Sp2001 c 10 art 2 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/379/#laws.1.7.0">2002 c 379 art 1 s 7</a>,8; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/1/#laws.2.34.0">1Sp2003 c 1 art 2 s 34</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/2/#laws.8.1.0">1Sp2003 c 2 art 8 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/156/#laws.2.15.0">2005 c 156 art 2 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.48.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 48</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/7/#laws.11.1.0">1Sp2011 c 7 art 11 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/0/63/#laws.1.1.0">2019 c 63 art 1 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/0/115/#laws.3.1.0">2020 c 115 art 3 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/7/#laws.5.1.0">1Sp2021 c 7 art 5 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/53/#laws.0.2.0">2022 c 53 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/25/#laws.0.3.0">2023 c 25 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/60/#laws.8.1.0">2023 c 60 art 8 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.4.4.0">2023 c 70 art 4 s 4</a></p>
      </div>
      <p><b>NOTE: </b>Subdivision 2, paragraph (k), as added by Laws 2023, chapter 70, article 4, section 4, is effective retroactively from April 1, 2023, and applies to settlement agreements or assurances of discontinuance entered into, or court orders issues on or after that date. Laws 2023, chapter 70, article 4, section 4, the effective date.</p>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16A.152">
        <h1 class="shn">16A.152 BUDGET RESERVE AND CASH FLOW ACCOUNTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.152.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Cash flow account established.</span></h2>
          <p>A cash flow account is created in the general fund in the state treasury. Amounts in the cash flow account shall remain in the account until drawn down and used to meet cash flow deficiencies resulting from uneven distribution of revenue collections and required expenditures during a fiscal year.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.152.1a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1a.<span class="headnote">Budget reserve.</span></h2>
          <p>A budget reserve account is created in the general fund in the state treasury. The commissioner of management and budget shall transfer to the budget reserve account on July 1 of each odd-numbered year any amounts specifically appropriated by law to the budget reserve.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.152.1b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1b.<span class="headnote">Budget reserve level.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner of management and budget shall calculate the budget reserve level by multiplying the current biennium's general fund nondedicated revenues and the most recent budget reserve percentage under subdivision 8.</p>
          <p>(b) If, on the basis of a November forecast of general fund revenues and expenditures, the commissioner of management and budget determines that there will be a positive unrestricted general fund balance at the close of the biennium and that the provisions of subdivision 2, paragraph (a), clauses (1), (2), (3), and (4), are satisfied, the commissioner shall transfer to the budget reserve account in the general fund the amount necessary to increase the budget reserve to the budget reserve level determined under paragraph (a). The amount of the transfer authorized in this paragraph shall not exceed 33 percent of the positive unrestricted general fund balance determined in the forecast.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.152.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Additional revenues; priority.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) If on the basis of a forecast of general fund revenues and expenditures, the commissioner of management and budget determines that there will be a positive unrestricted budgetary general fund balance at the close of the biennium, the commissioner of management and budget must allocate money to the following accounts and purposes in priority order:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) the cash flow account established in subdivision 1 until that account reaches $350,000,000;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) the budget reserve account established in subdivision 1a until that account reaches $2,852,098,000;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) the amount necessary to increase the aid payment schedule for school district aids and credits payments in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/127A.45">127A.45</a> to not more than 90 percent rounded to the nearest tenth of a percent without exceeding the amount available and with any remaining funds deposited in the budget reserve; and</p>
          <p class="in">(4) the amount necessary to restore all or a portion of the net aid reductions under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/127A.441">127A.441</a> and to reduce the property tax revenue recognition shift under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.75#stat.123B.75.5">123B.75, subdivision 5</a>, by the same amount.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) The amounts necessary to meet the requirements of this section are appropriated from the general fund within two weeks after the forecast is released or, in the case of transfers under paragraph (a), clauses (3) and (4), as necessary to meet the appropriations schedules otherwise established in statute.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) The commissioner of management and budget shall certify the total dollar amount of the reductions under paragraph (a), clauses (3) and (4), to the commissioner of education. The commissioner of education shall increase the aid payment percentage and reduce the property tax shift percentage by these amounts and apply those reductions to the current fiscal year and thereafter.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.152.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Use.</span></h2>
          <p>The use of the budget reserve should be governed by principles based on the full economic cycle rather than the budget cycle. The budget reserve may be used when a negative budgetary balance is projected and when objective measures, such as reduced growth in total wages, retail sales, or employment, reflect downturns in the state's economy.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.152.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Reduction.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) If the commissioner determines that probable receipts for the general fund will be less than anticipated, and that the amount available for the remainder of the biennium will be less than needed, the commissioner shall, with the approval of the governor, and after consulting the Legislative Advisory Commission, reduce the amount in the budget reserve account as needed to balance expenditures with revenue.</p>
          <p>(b) An additional deficit shall, with the approval of the governor, and after consulting the Legislative Advisory Commission, be made up by reducing unexpended allotments of any prior appropriation or transfer. Notwithstanding any other law to the contrary, the commissioner is empowered to defer or suspend prior statutorily created obligations which would prevent effecting such reductions.</p>
          <p>(c) If the commissioner determines that probable receipts for any other fund, appropriation, or item will be less than anticipated, and that the amount available for the remainder of the term of the appropriation or for any allotment period will be less than needed, the commissioner shall notify the agency concerned and then reduce the amount allotted or to be allotted so as to prevent a deficit.</p>
          <p>(d) In reducing allotments, the commissioner may consider other sources of revenue available to recipients of state appropriations and may apply allotment reductions based on all sources of revenue available.</p>
          <p>(e) In like manner, the commissioner shall reduce allotments to an agency by the amount of any saving that can be made over previous spending plans through a reduction in prices or other cause.</p>
          <p>(f) The commissioner is prohibited from reducing an allotment or appropriation made to the legislature.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.152.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Restoration.</span></h2>
          <p>The restoration of the budget reserve should be governed by principles based on the full economic cycle rather than the budget cycle. Restoration of the budget reserve should occur when objective measures, such as increased growth in total wages, retail sales, or employment, reflect upturns in the state's economy. The budget reserve should be restored before new or increased spending commitments are made.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.152.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Notice to committees.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall notify the committees on finance and taxes and tax laws of the senate and the committees on ways and means and taxes of the house of representatives of a reduction in an allotment under this section. The notice must be in writing and delivered within 15 days of the commissioner's act. The notice must specify:</p>
          <p>(1) the amount of the reduction in the allotment;</p>
          <p>(2) the agency and programs affected;</p>
          <p>(3) the amount of any payment withheld; and</p>
          <p>(4) any additional information the commissioner determines is appropriate.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.152.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Delay; reduction.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may delay paying up to 15 percent of an appropriation to a special taxing district or a system of higher education in that entity's fiscal year for up to 60 days after the start of its next fiscal year. The delayed amount is subject to allotment reduction under subdivision 4.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.152.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Report on budget reserve percentage.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) The commissioner of management and budget shall develop and annually review a methodology for evaluating the adequacy of the budget reserve based on the volatility of Minnesota's general fund tax structure. The review must take into consideration relevant statistical and economic literature. After completing the review, the commissioner may revise the methodology if necessary. The commissioner must use the methodology to annually estimate the percentage of the current biennium's general fund nondedicated revenues recommended as a budget reserve.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) By September 30 of each year, the commissioner shall report the percentage of the current biennium's general fund nondedicated revenue that is recommended as a budget reserve to the chairs and ranking minority members of the senate Committee on Finance, the house of representatives Committee on Ways and Means, and the senate and house of representatives Committees on Taxes. The report must also specify:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) whether the commissioner revised the recommendation as a result of significant changes in the mix of general fund taxes or the base of one or more general fund taxes;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) whether the commissioner revised the recommendation as a result of a revision to the methodology; and</p>
          <p class="in">(3) any additional appropriate information.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16A.152.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>

      <div class="sr" id="stat.16A.1523"><b>16A.1523</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/220/#laws.13.11.0">2002 c 220 art 13 s 11</a>]</div>

      <div class="sr" id="stat.16A.154"><b>16A.154</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/1/1/#laws.5.12.0">1Sp1986 c 1 art 5 s 12</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.16A.1541"><b>16A.1541</b> [Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.152">16A.152, subd 2</a>]
</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16A.155">
        <h1 class="shn">16A.155 REFUNDS; CHARGED WHEN PAID.</h1>
        <p>Notwithstanding sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.14">16A.14</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.15">16A.15</a>, or any other law to the contrary, the payment of a refund shall be charged to the fund, appropriation, allotment or encumbrance for the period in which the refund is paid.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.16A.155.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/231/#laws.0.11.0">1976 c 231 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/628/#laws.2.1.0">1984 c 628 art 2 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.16A.16"><b>16A.16</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/299/#laws.0.36.0">1983 c 299 s 36</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.16A.17">
        <h1 class="shn">16A.17 PREPARATION OF STATE PAYROLL.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.17.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Salaries; when paid.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner, with the approval of the governor, may choose to pay salaried employees semimonthly or biweekly.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.16A.17.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
